# Use-Case Verification — `deliverables/ui-mockup/index.html`

**Scope:** the clickable PatternFly 6 mock of the proposed MTA console improvements.
**Baseline:** `deliverables/ui-mockup/index.html` as of November 2026 (post IA consolidation).
**Reference catalog:** [`evidence/mta-improvement-suggestions.md`](../../evidence/mta-improvement-suggestions.md) — suggestion numbers `#N` below cite it; the mock renders the same numbers as annotation badges — hidden by default (user request: inline badges otherwise widen buttons and wrap column headers), revealed by the masthead **Annotations** switch or `#anno=1` in the URL; the per-screen "Suggestions" index button works in both modes

---

## 1. Purpose, method, and metrics

This document walks the mock's implemented flows as **use cases per persona** (the mock's own IA
principle, #36: nav items are entities and daily workflows; everything else is tabs, drawers,
modals, or saved views). For each use case it records the verification status **and four product
metrics**:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Steps** | concrete reproduction path in the mock |
| **Expected** | the behavior the underlying suggestions require |
| **Suggestions** | which catalog items the flow exercises |
| **Status** | ✅ verified (automated) · 👁 verified (manual review) · ⚠ partial / mock-only |
| **Achievable** | **Y** — the journey can be walked end-to-end in the mock · **Y\*** — navigable and fully presented, but the concluding action is intentionally inert (the mock presents the surface; it does not change state) · **N** — cannot be walked |
| **Clicks** | interactions (clicks; Enter = 1) from mock boot to the state that fulfills the use case. Boot persona is **architect**, boot screen is **Home**; journeys in another persona's landing include the persona-switch click. Measures navigation cost — the "#36/#41: one click to the work" claim. |
| **Data complete** | **Full** — everything needed to act on the use case is presented · **Partial** — presented with gaps (top-N samples, missing editors, inert concluding actions) |
| **Data quality** | **High** — internally consistent across surfaces (counts, dates, IDs reconcile; dates coherent with "today") · **Medium** — minor inconsistencies or sample-scale simplifications, documented per row |

**How it was verified.** Two harnesses were used, both against the real PatternFly 6.7.0 stylesheet:

1. **Automated (jsdom):** loads `index.html`, drives clicks and hash changes programmatically,
   asserts DOM state (modal visibility, filter results, tab panels, chip rendering, ARIA state).
   Covers behavior, not layout.
2. **Automated (headless Chrome):** same assertions plus *computed layout* (rendered heights,
   `display` values) — catches what jsdom cannot, e.g. PF6 CSS states like `:not(.pf-m-expanded)`.
3. **Automated (dead-end audit):** a DOM walk over every screen, modal, and drawer template that
   finds *entity references rendered as plain text* — application names, archetype names, rule
   paths, tracker keys — and checks whether the reference is clickable or a dead-end. Wired
   references are then click-tested (including `stopPropagation` inside clickable rows, so the
   reference navigates to *its* target while the row still opens the row's own view). The same
   audit covers the **navigation chrome**: every tab (16) is clicked and its panel asserted
   visible, every nav item (6) and wizard step (5) is click-tested to its target, and every
   pagination control is either wired (Applications) or fully `disabled` (static samples). It also
   runs **at runtime**: every drawer/modal variant is opened (all 12 app-drawer states, the seven
   side drawers, the comparison view) and every rendered table row is checked for clickability —
   catching content assembled by JavaScript that a static template scan would miss.

The jsdom suites themselves live in the workspace at [`verify/`](verify) — 19 suites plus the
shared `inv-rows-reference-wp3.json` fixture, with a per-suite index, run instructions, and the
full list of jsdom limits in [`verify/README.md`](verify/README.md). They resolve jsdom via
`require('jsdom')` (after `npm install` inside `verify/`) with a fallback to the historical
ephemeral `/tmp/node_modules` location. The §4 defect-log rows below cite `/tmp/opencode/`
paths as they were recorded at the time; the current canonical location is `verify/`.

> ⚠ Known harness limitation: jsdom does not execute inline `onclick` handlers on content cloned
> from `<template>` elements. That is a jsdom gap, not a mock defect — real browsers execute them
> (confirmed with a minimal repro). All template-originated flows were therefore verified via
> headless Chrome or attribute inspection.
>
> ⚠ Harness note (drawer investigation, 2026-11-12): clicking the invoicing row appeared to "not
> open the drawer" in both harnesses. Root cause was in the test, not the mock — twice over:
> (a) `app2` is in the mock's `MODAL_VIEWS` set, so the row routes to the application detail
> **modal** (`#modalApp`), while the harness asserted on the side-drawer elements (`#drawerRoot`/
> `#drawerBody`); asserting on `#modalApp` shows the click works in jsdom and Chrome alike.
> (b) jsdom additionally fires a `hashchange` → `applyHash` → `show()` round-trip on drawer opens
> that real browsers don't reproduce, so *any* drawer-state assertion made synchronously in jsdom
> is timing-dependent — drawer paths are Chrome-verified only. Separately, a jsdom-only ordering
> artifact means drawer opens via row click must be asserted after the hashchange settles. No
> product change resulted from this investigation; a similar false alarm was pre-empted for the
> default `wave3` filter chip, which is statically `on` at boot (a new unassigned app is hidden
> until the chip is cleared — same as upstream, where an active filter hides new rows).

---

## 2. Persona journeys

### 2.0 RBAC — how the personas are enforced (user request)

Personas are not cosmetic: a `resource:verb` scope model (`RBAC` in the source) drives **navigation,
deep links, and in-page verbs**. `admin` holds every scope; work personas get the narrow set their
job needs.

**Screen visibility (views list per persona):**

- **architect** — Home, Applications, Incidents, Waves, Reports, Agentic, Import wizard
- **pm** — Home, Applications, Incidents, Waves, Reports, Task manager
- **pgm** — the same read-only view set as pm (the difference is tenancy scope — below and §2.0.1)
- **migrator** — Home, Applications, Incidents, Waves, Agentic, Task manager, Import wizard
- **admin** — everything, plus the Administration hub · **padmin** — the same views (UC-X17)

**Who sees Reports, and why** (user question "who should be able to see reports?"): five of the six
personas — architect, pm, pgm, admin, padmin; the **migrator does not**. The rationale: the
migrator's surfaces are *queues and records* (Agentic run tables, Task manager, Incidents) — its own
runs' state, event logs, and cost accounting live on the run record itself, while Reports aggregates
(showback, trends, risk rollups) answer portfolio questions for the people who own the portfolio.
The deep-link guard (`#scr=reports` as migrator → Home + toast) is pinned in the RBAC suite. The
question also exposed that Reports → Agent runs was the one report surface not tenancy-clamped
(static KPIs contradicting the records) — now the whole tab computes from the scoped store (UC-X16).

**Tenancy scope — Project manager vs Program manager vs Migrator (#41):** the Project manager and
the Program manager share the same read-only view set but differ in *tenancy scope* — the **Project
manager** belongs to a team (Claims) and is **locked to it** (the masthead 🏢 switcher disables with
a tooltip pointing at the Program manager persona; `&org=`/`&team=` deep-link params lose to the
lock), while the **Program manager** keeps the free organization/department scope and additionally
sees the **cross-team coordination view** on Migration waves (§2.0.1) plus a program-item in the
notification feed. The **Migrator is team-locked too** (same home team, Claims): it keeps its
execution verbs (applications CRUD, incident triage/resolve, run creation, task cancel) but
exercises them within the team that owns the applications — the lock works exactly like the Project
manager's (switcher disabled with a tooltip, deep-link params lose).

**Credential governance (#15 + #41, user request):** credentials are **team-owned resources** —
every credential belongs to exactly one team (the team whose repositories consume it), the same
ownership invariant as applications — and management follows the tenancy clamp: the **architect and
migrator** hold `identities:create / edit / verify / delete` for *their team's* credentials, the
**admin personas** for the whole organization (platform admin: cross-org, audited); pm/pgm hold
none.

**Credentials is a first-class view** (promoted out of the Administration hub — the WP6 "the hub
holds configuration, not operations" precedent): `scopedCreds()` is the only credential-list source,
the table shows the current scope's credentials with an **Owner team** column, `#scr=credentials`
and the legacy `#scr=config&tab=credentials` both land there, and the hub retains 8 tabs.

**Readiness is a wave GATE condition, not a separate card** (user challenge "do we really need a
pre-wave check card on Migration waves?" — answered: the *check* yes, the *card* no): credential
readiness appears in the wave's **Gate status** list (#46 definition-of-done) — Wave 3 names the
expiring `svn-read-key` and the Wave-3 members it serves, and notes the unverified credential is
*unassigned* (no gate impact) — visible to **everyone gating the wave** (gate *state*, not
credential *management*: no secrets, no verbs; management stays `identities:*` on the Credentials
view). The data settled the argument: the card's headline failure (unverified `nexus-mvn`) gated
nothing (used by 0 applications), while the real Wave-3 risk — an expiring credential used by two
members — read as one static table row among three.

**Assignment at creation (#15 + #41):** the application form's **Source credential** select offers
exactly the *selected team's* credentials (it repopulates when the Team select changes; a team
without credentials gets a guided none-option), edit preselects the assigned credential, the
application detail names it (both the parameterized and the canonical template paths follow
reassignments), and the "Used by" counts on the Credentials view recompute from the assignments —
so creating an app with a credential updates the blast radius everywhere. The import wizard's
Credentials step (#44/R2-15) offers the owning team's credentials, filtered to the scheme's kinds.

**Verb summary** (full matrix below): **architect** = applications CRUD + queue analysis, wave
re-validation (`waves:edit`), review sign-off (`review:write`), agent-run creation (`runs:create`)
and cancellation of running runs (`runs:cancel`), credential management within the tenancy clamp
(`identities:*`) · **pm** = none (read-only by design) · **migrator** = applications CRUD + queue
analysis, incident triage/resolve, agent-run creation, **cancellation of running runs**
(`runs:cancel` — the executor can stop a run that is burning quota or gate time, user request),
task cancel, credential management within the team (`identities:*`) · **admin** = `*`.

**Enforcement:** nav links and the masthead task shortcut hide per view list; gated buttons disable
with the missing scope on hover; deep links to disallowed screens redirect Home with a warning
toast; the notification feed (#36 item 5) filters to the active role (its masthead bell renders as
a fa-bell icon + unread badge per user request — it previously rendered as a bare badge with no
icon).

**Related sections:** **Admin tiers (#41/#42, user request)** — the **Admin** is an *organization*
admin, org-locked like every other persona (the 🏢 switcher's organization entries disable; `&org=`
deep links lose to the lock; the Teams table shows only its own organization), while the **Platform
administrator** (`padmin`) operates *above* the tenancy model (UC-X17) — detailed in §2.0.2.
**Architect responsibility levels (#41 + #36, user request)** — the architect additionally carries a
**responsibility level** (application / team / department / organization) that clamps the tenancy
scope (UC-X18) — detailed in §2.0.3.

Verified by `verify/verify-rbac.js` (171 checks).

**Verb matrix.** The model is verb-based: each persona holds a list of `resource:verb` scopes
(the `RBAC` const — one source of truth for nav, deep-link guards, and in-page verbs); `can(verb, res)`
checks membership, with `scopes: '*'` as the wildcard. Every gated control in the mock carries
`data-rbac="resource:verb"` and shows the missing scope on hover. The full matrix:

| Resource | Verb (control it gates) | Architect | Project mgr | Program mgr | Migrator | Admin (org) | Platform admin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| applications | `create` / `edit` / `delete` (toolbar + row actions) | ✓ | – | – | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| applications | `analyze` (Queue analysis) | ✓ | – | – | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| waves | `edit` (re-validate / plan) | ✓ | – | – | – | ✓ | ✓ |
| review | `write` (assessment/review sign-off) | ✓ | – | – | – | ✓ | ✓ |
| incidents | `triage` / `resolve` | – | – | – | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| runs (agent runs) | `create` | ✓ | – | – | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| runs (agent runs) | `cancel` (Cancel on running workflow/agent runs) | ✓ | – | – | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| runs (agent runs) | `pause` (Stop ⏸ / Resume ▶ on running/paused workflow + agent runs — one `runs:pause` scope, held with `cancel`) | ✓ | – | – | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| runs (agent runs) | `delete` ("Delete finished (N)" + workflow-run row deletes) | – | – | – | – | ✓ | ✓ |
| agents (registry) | `create` | – | – | – | – | ✓ | ✓ |
| agents (registry) | `delete` (agent/skill/workflow row delete) | – | – | – | – | ✓ | ✓ |
| tasks | `cancel` (bulk cancel) | – | – | – | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| identities | `create` (Add credential) | ✓ ² | – | – | ✓ ² | ✓ | ✓ |
| identities | `edit` (Replace key / rotate) | ✓ ² | – | – | ✓ ² | ✓ | ✓ |
| identities | `verify` (re-test) | ✓ ² | – | – | ✓ ² | ✓ | ✓ |
| identities | `delete` (Revoke) | ✓ ² | – | – | ✓ ² | ✓ | ✓ |
| identities | `read` (Credentials view; the waves' credentials *gate state* is visible to everyone) | ✓ | – | – | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| teams | `create` / `delete` (Controls → Teams) | – | – | – | – | ✓ | ✓ |
| tenants | `read` / `create` (Organizations tab, provisioning) | – | – | – | – | – **¹** | ✓ |

¹ `can(verb, 'tenants')` short-circuits to the Platform administrator *before* the wildcard is
consulted, so the org admin's `scopes: '*'` deliberately does **not** imply tenant verbs — the
platform layer cannot be widened by a wildcard (UC-X17). Project manager and Program manager hold
no verbs at all (`scopes: []`) — read-only by design; the Program manager's added value is scope
(cross-team view, §2.0.1), not verbs. Screen visibility (`canView`) is the views list per persona,
quoted in the prose above; row-level verb gating is re-applied after every re-render
(`applyRbac()` on boot, persona switch, and each render path).
² Credential verbs are **scope-clamped, not global** (UC-X19): the architect/migrator manage only
the credentials owned by their tenancy clamp's teams — the verb grants the *capability*, the
tenancy clamp grants the *object*.

### 2.0.1 Multi-tenancy — organizations, departments, teams (#41, user request)

The scope model (`TENANCY` in the source) implements the user's definition: **organizations are
isolated** — switching one changes every application, wave, and aggregate you can see — each
organization has **departments**, departments have **teams**, every application belongs to
**exactly one team** (a team may own many), and a team is staffed with **architects, project
managers, and migrators**.

**How the scope drives the UI:** `state.org/dept/team` is the scope: the masthead switcher
(🏢 Northwind Financial · Retail Banking · all teams) opens one cascading org → department → team
menu with member counts; `scopedApps()` is the only application-list source, so team scope filters
the Applications rows and the wave membership/effort/risk aggregates cascade automatically. Deep
links carry the scope (`&org=&dept=&team=`).

**Waves belong to organizations:** Waves 1–4 are Northwind-owned — other organizations see a guided
empty state (#43) pointing at the wave-assignment path, and Home/Reports carry a "data scope" note.

**Teams management (Administration → Controls):** a **Teams** management table (the hierarchy with
the three role columns and live application counts) with `teams:create`/`teams:delete` admin-only
and the established guarded delete — a team with assigned applications cannot be deleted; the block
reason names the count and the exactly-one-team invariant. The app form's Team select (grouped by
department) enforces exactly-one-team on create and edit.

**The persona × tenancy intersection (user request):** the **Project manager** and **Migrator**
personas are locked to their home team (Claims — switcher disabled with a tooltip, deep-link scope
params lose to the lock); the **Program manager** persona keeps free org/department scope and gets
the **cross-team coordination info rendered in each wave** (user request "move the coordination
info into each wave" — the separate pgm-only card duplicated the wave's own context one section
away and is gone): each wave's detail opens with its coordination strip — contributing teams with a
Cross-team flag when the wave spans more than one team (sampled: Wave 4 = Claims + Portfolio +
Customer digital), the wave's own quota blockers (#38) naming the teams they hit, and the wave's
side of the inter-wave dependency edge with its team-boundary verdict (Wave 3: edge out, stays
within Claims; Wave 4: edge in) — while a team-scoped Project manager sees no strips (the info
names other teams by definition). The **program landing on Home keeps the summary** (teams in
scope / cross-team waves / shared blockers KPIs), a program item in the notification feed, and its
own guided tour.

**Sampled data:** Northwind Financial keeps the 12 original apps (byte-equality preserved);
Meridian Health adds 3 isolated apps (patient-records, lab-results, appointment-api).

Verified by `verify/verify-tenancy.js` (49 checks).

### 2.0.2 Admin tiers — organization admin vs platform administrator (#41 + #42, user request)

Admin is split so the isolation invariant holds for every persona inside a tenant, with exactly one
explicit, audited way across it:

- **Admin = organization admin (tenant-side).** Same capabilities as before, but scoped to *its*
  organization: the 🏢 switcher's organization entries render disabled (departments and teams remain
  selectable), `&org=` deep-link params lose to the lock, `setOrg` is a guarded no-op, and the
  Controls → Teams table lists only the current organization's teams. Nothing about the admin
  persona pierces another tenant anymore. Its **Home landing answers an organization question** —
  "Is my organization's migration on track?" — not a platform one: the original "Is the platform
  healthy?" landing (static queue-depth/failed-tasks/verification KPIs + a Platform-health card)
  predated the admin-tier split, and the platform framing moved to the platform admin's
  tenant-portfolio landing above. The rebuilt landing is **org-scoped and live** (`scopedApps()` IS
  its organization): applications, wave-assigned, open incidents, agent spend (#20), expiring
  credentials (#15), plus an Organization-status list with deep links (failing Wave-3 gate, risk
  distribution, credentials, task queue) — refreshed on tenancy/CRUD changes, and stable under the
  guarded `setOrg` (Chrome shotOA).
- **Platform administrator (`padmin`) = operator-side, above tenancy.** The only persona with
  `tenants:*` verbs (`can('…','tenants')` is exclusive to it, so `scopes: '*'` no longer implies
  them). It gets: a **tenant-portfolio Home landing** (user question "what could be useful on the
  home screen for a platform admin?" — its tour question "Which organizations need attention?"
  previously had no landing at all, only a tour routed to Controls; a follow-up request added the
  **platform-health section** the org-admin reframe had removed — now fleet-wide where it
  belongs): tenancy-footprint KPIs
  (organizations / teams / applications, live from the `TENANCY` store), a **per-organization
  status table with an attention verdict** (apps, wave-assigned, fleet-wide agent spend #20 —
  computed above the clamp on purpose, expiring credentials #15, and a verdict: expiring
  credentials → onboarding → healthy), a **Platform-health
  section** (analysis pipeline, tracker sync #37, fleet-wide credential health #15 computed live
  across all organizations — each org admin sees only its own — plus queue-depth #30, failed-tasks
  #3/#38, and verification-pass-rate #47 KPIs), the **cross-tenant audit trail up front** (#42 —
  this persona's own accountability surface), and a `tenants:create` **Provision organization**
  shortcut; the orgs table, the audit trail, and this landing share one renderer, so provisioning
  and audit entries refresh all three (the 4th tour step now leads with it). Plus: the
  **Organizations** sub-tab in Administration → Controls (org table with
  department/team/application counts + **Add organization** provisioning modal with required-name,
  duplicate, and required-first-department validation), the **cross-tenant audit trail** (#42:
  time, actor, action, target — seeded with the Meridian provisioning entry), a program item in the
  notification feed, a 4-step guided tour, and cross-org movement: switching organization works
  only for this persona and each pivot appends a `tenant:access` audit entry *and* raises a warning
  toast naming the tenant — cross-org reads are deliberate and recorded, never ambient. Deep links
  with `&org=` also audit for this persona.

Verified by `verify/verify-rbac.js` (the "admin tiers" block) and the org-lock deep-link
checks in `verify/verify-tenancy.js`.

### 2.0.3 Architect responsibility levels — application · team · department · organization (#41 + #36, user request)

The architect is not one free-roaming role: an architect is **responsible for a scope**, in four
levels. A 📎 **Responsibility** selector renders in the masthead (beside the 🏢 switcher) **only
while the Architect persona is active**:

- **Application architect** — responsible for one application (sampled: `payments-api`); sees only
  its row, its analysis, its incidents, and its wave lane (Wave 3) — the tenancy switcher disables
  with a tooltip pointing at the responsibility level as the way out, and the masthead scope label
  gains an `· app payments-api` suffix.
- **Team architect** — responsible for one team (Claims): the team's 6 applications and their wave
  lanes; the switcher disables (same lock pattern as the Project manager/Migrator).
- **Department architect** — responsible for one department (Retail Banking): the department is
  locked (`setDept` to another department is a guarded no-op and the menu renders the other
  department disabled), but **teams remain selectable** within it.
- **Organization architect** (default, boot state) — responsible for the whole organization: the
  organization is locked (like the admin; `setOrg` to another organization is a no-op and Meridian
  renders disabled in the menu), departments and teams remain selectable.

The level is a **clamp, not a separate dataset**: it narrows what the existing tenancy engine may
select (`archClampScope()`), and every surface — Applications rows, wave lanes, aggregates, Home
KPIs, Reports — follows through `scopedApps()` as usual. Lowering the level locks further
(app/team engage the team lock); raising it keeps the current position but re-enables movement
(widening is explicit). The level is deep-linkable with `&arch=app|team|dept|org`; deep-link
`&org=` params still lose to the architect's organization clamp. Each level explains itself: the
selector's menu note (#41 + #36) and the per-item descriptions name the responsibility and what
visibility it grants.

Verified by `verify/verify-rbac.js` (the "architect responsibility levels" block: selector
visibility per persona, dept no-op with teams free, team lock, app single-row + W3-only lane,
deep-link clamp, level-switch switcher refresh) + Chrome screenshot (open Responsibility menu).

### 2.1 Migrator — "What is assigned to me?"

*Persona switch from boot adds 1 click to the migrator journeys (boot persona is architect).*

| ID | Use case | Steps | Expected | Suggestions | Status | Achievable | Clicks | Data complete | Data quality |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| UC-M1 | Assigned work from Home opens the work item directly | Home (migrator) → click a "My work" row | Incident detail **modal** opens in one click from the landing — rule + file (#8), migration hint (#45), triage history with actor/timestamps (#33 item 1, #26), tracker sync (#37), role-gated verbs **now enforced by the RBAC scopes** (UC-X15): Mark resolved and Mark false positive render for the migrator and are disabled for architect/pm, Open file and Assign to agent group stay informational (#41) | #36 item 1, #41 | ✅ automated | Y | 2 | Full | High — the same incident (WebLogic JNDI lookup, invoicing, 5 pts, MIG-412 synced) reconciles across Home, the Incidents queue, and the modal |
| UC-M2 | Work an incident to completion | UC-M1 → use the verbs; check the triage-state table | Triage decisions writable and auditable (#33 item 1); false-positive marking feeds the rules loop (#6); hint verified against the cohort charter (#45) | #6, #33 item 1, #26, #45 | 👁 manual | Y* | 2 (+1 inert verb click) | Full | High — history entries carry actor, date, origin surface ("via IDE extension"); the write itself is intentionally inert in the mock |
| UC-M3 | Open and filter the full queue | Home → "Open my queue in Incidents →"; pick a State; set/remove the application chip | Incidents screen with the "assigned to me" default view (#2); the State menu (All/Triage/In progress/Assigned) **actually filters the rows**, the app filter chip scopes to one application (#41) and combines with the state filter; guided empty state when nothing matches (#43) | #2, #41, #43 | ✅ automated | Y | 2 | Full | High — representative sample with working filters; empty state states the way out |
| UC-M4 | Drill into an application | Applications → click any row | App detail modal: actions row (Queue analysis, Compare with previous run), metrics row (Effort, Risk, Analysis, Last analysis), detail tabs — the Dependencies tab renders the application's **actual artifact table** (version ranges, conflicts, transitive depth, CVE signals) for the sampled apps, with a hand-off link to the full Dependencies view | #33 item 8, #32 item 6, #24 | ✅ automated | Y | 2 | Partial — full artifact inventories are sampled for payments-api (5 of 214) and invoicing (2); other apps hand off to the Dependencies view | High — the drawer table is cloned live from the Dependencies view, so the two surfaces cannot disagree |
| UC-M5 | See an app's incidents in context | App modal → "Related incidents" tab → click an incident row (or "open in Incidents →") | Per-app incident groups with effort and tracker sync; **each incident row opens the incident detail parameterized to the clicked incident** — application, effort, triage state, tracker outcome, and a derived rule path all come from the row (same assemble-from-the-row pattern as the drawer itself); hand-off to the queue, app-filtered | #4, #37, #41 | ✅ automated | Y | 3 (+1 optional) | Partial by design — "top 3 of 137" style sampling, honestly labeled, with the queue carrying the rest | High — totals reconcile with the Applications row (214 incidents / 1,180 pts for payments-api), and the detail modal now matches the row clicked |

### 2.2 Architect — "Where is the portfolio hurting?"

*Boot persona; no switch needed.*

| ID | Use case | Steps | Expected | Suggestions | Status | Achievable | Clicks | Data complete | Data quality |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

in the duration cell on agent runs — aria-label "Cancel run", `runs:cancel` — architect/migrator/admin): cancellation is a lifecycle **state change, not a deletion** — the record is kept with its partial token/cost accounting (#42 posture), the reason names who cancelled and that the quota slot is freed (#38), and cancelled runs land in the admin-only Delete-finished set; **cancellation propagates through the lifecycle** (one agent run = one stage, user question "how would cancelling an agent run affect a workflow run?"): cancelling a stage cancels its parent workflow run — a workflow cannot proceed past a cancelled stage, and the parent's reason names the stage — and cancelling a workflow run cancels its running/queued stages (succeeded stages keep their outcome); every affected event log gets a terminal `stage CANCELLED — by <persona>` event, so the log survives the state change (#50); **stop/resume** (user request): a running run can also be **stopped** — a checkpoint pause, not a cancellation: the workspace branch and its checkpoints persist (git is the persistence layer), model quota spend halts, the state reads Paused, and **Resume** continues from the last checkpoint (no checkpointed work repeated) — stop/resume propagate through the lifecycle exactly like cancellation (stage ↔ parent), log `run stopped`/`run resumed` events, a paused run is *not* in the Delete-finished set, and a cancelled run is *not* resumable (cancel stays the terminal abandon action) — *Update (user question — 'logs of a running agent'):* the run record carries a **structured event log** — the raw model transcript is ephemeral by upstream design (workspaces ephemeral, git persists), so observability comes from events: stage start/handoff, tool calls, model calls with token counts, sandbox denials (OCSF, #16 item 6), checkpoints, and the failure cause (#3); a running record renders it as a **live tail** ('events so far'), finished records keep it frozen; **every stage record carries its event log** (a user report after the modal transition read the old sampling as 'the log got lost' — universal now: live tail while running, frozen afterwards, terminal event = the failure cause), and the workflow-run modal's stage cards show each stage's latest event; rows carrying a log are **badged #50** in the Agent-runs table (and the Reports rollup), so the access path is discoverable without opening rows blind; its durability (write-through, retention, export) is **Suggestion #50**|

remove the membership there first (Teams tab)"), so the delete order is wave-reference guard → team-staffing guard → cascade; and the Teams tab now *operates* the rosters (user request): an Edit-members modal per team (pen icon, `teams:edit` scope) with role-grouped member check-lists — the people pool is the Stakeholders list (the boot derivation's source), one role per person per team (checking a name under another role moves it), remove = uncheck; the round trip closes the loop the reverse guard opened: removing T. Baumann onto a team makes his guarded — and a person removed from all teams keeps their stakeholder record (staffing was the boot source, not a lifetime binding)** (user question "how do stakeholders and stakeholder groups map?"; live instance 2026-11-12: the group carries name + description + stakeholders[], e.g. "JBoss EAP Quickstarts — stakeholders [John Doe]"): the group record owns its member list (name/description/member check-list modal, Add/Edit/two-step delete), the Stakeholder table's Groups column *derives* from group membership (single source of truth — a group rename or membership change re-renders the stakeholder rows live, no parallel display string to drift), and a stakeholder with no group shows an em-dash until assigned. The bsvc-owner, app-owner and team-roster pickers all enforce it; groups' member check-lists remain cross-org — groups are not org-scoped records (they bind to waves, which are global), recorded honestly here rather than silently leaked. Groups' member check-lists remain cross-org — groups are not org-scoped records (they bind to waves, which are global), recorded honestly here rather than silently leaked. **Descriptions are equally invariant** (user requirement "a business service should have a description"): boot sample fully described (Pricing — previously an empty description with an em-dash table cell — now "Premium and rate calculations"), the modal **requires** one ("Description is required — say what the service covers (it drives grouping, reporting and wave scope)"), clearing the field on edit is refused the same way, and the em-dash Description placeholder is now a never-fires-at-boot tripwire like the Unowned chip. **Department CRUD now exists as its own Controls sub-tab** (user question "where are the CRUD operations for departments?" — previously departments were only born implicitly with an organization and had no management surface): Organization/Department/Teams/Applications table with Add/Edit/guarded-delete — org admins manage their own organization's departments under a dedicated `depts:*` scope family (NOT `tenants:*`, which `can()` reserves as Platform-administrator-exclusive — that mis-scoping briefly disabled the buttons for the org admin and tripped the RBAC suite), the Platform administrator sees all organizations; **UC-AD5 update history (chronological):**

- *Update (user question on consolidating the two run tabs):* **kept as two tabs, now linked** — they are one lifecycle at two granularities (orchestration vs. accounting record; a merged table would either bloat columns or hide token/cost). A declared parent field (`wr`) links agent runs to their workflow run: a workflow run's detail lists **"Agent runs composing this workflow run"** (click-through jumps to the Agent-runs record), and an agent run's detail links back to **"Parent workflow run"** (or says "Ad-hoc run — no parent workflow-run record").
- *Update (user request):* clicking a run row — workflow **or agent** — opens the detail as a **modal** (`pf-v6-c-modal-box`, app-detail precedent; the drawer is retired from run records): the jump links chain modals (a stage-card link in the workflow-run modal opens the agent-run record modal, the parent link opens the workflow-run modal — or WR-8's rich record), so the whole lifecycle reads in one surface.
- *Update (user rule):* **an agent run is always part of a workflow run** — the parent link is mandatory, pinned as a data invariant (every agent run has a `wr` parent, every workflow run carries its stage-level agent runs, and parent/child agree on the application); the "Ad-hoc run" escape hatch was removed and four previously parentless records gained declared parents (three via new workflow-run records WR-15/WR-11/WR-10/WR-9).
- *Update (user request):* the agent runs' **information now lives in the workflow-run modal** — one record card per composing run with identity, agent, phase, state, duration, failure cause (#3) and the token/cost/gateway/image accounting (#20/#28), plus the jump link to the full Agent-runs record (canonical #21 keeps proposal review + sandbox story), closed by a **run-total line** (#20: summed agent-run count, prompt/completion split and est. cost across the composing records — WR-13 demonstrates a real multi-record sum: #17 plan + #16 execute → 1M/96k, $30.90).
- *Update (user request):* the composing records are **rendered as a pipeline** — phase-ordered nodes (plan → execute → verify) joined by arrow connectors, each node state-colored (green/red/blue/orange left border + label) and clickable through to the full record; the Agent-runs tab remains the evidence/records list.
- *Update (user model correction — one agent run = ONE stage of a workflow run):* the plan/execute/verify **triplet record was decomposed** into three single-stage agent runs (#23/#24/#25), and the rich canonical record (per-stage token table, proposal review, sandbox envelope, OCSF events) moved to the **workflow-run level** — it now belongs to WR-8, a completed run of the same workflow, and opens from the WR-8 row click (and from the stage-table links); WR-14 (running) carries its own stage records (#26 plan succeeded → #27 execute running); queueing an on-demand agent run creates its parent workflow run with it, so the parent invariant holds at runtime

## 4. Defects found and fixed during verification

Verification worked — these were caught by the two harnesses (or by review prompted by it) and fixed: Rows predating the four-column format keep their whole defect → root cause → fix narrative verbatim in one cell, with — in the unused columns.

| # | Defect | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sort indicator rendered *below* header text (all 7 sortable columns) | PF6 sets `.pf-v6-c-table__text{display:block}` inside sort headers; mock lacked the `__button-content` inline-grid wrapper the React table renders | Added `pf-v6-c-table__button-content` wrapper to all 7 sort buttons |
| 2 | Only 1 of 10 filter category boxes rendered | `[hidden]` overridden by PF display rules | `#invChips [hidden]{display:none !important}` |

| 35 | **The notifications drawer’s close icon did nothing** (user report) — worse than nothing: clicking it re-OPENED the drawer. Root cause: `toggleDrawer()` called `closeMenus()` first, and `closeMenus()` unconditionally hides `notifDrawer` — the subsequent `m.hidden = !m.hidden` flipped the just-hidden state back to open, so the close icon toggled the drawer open. The bell handler had already worked around this by snapshotting visibility before `closeMenus()`; the fix makes `toggleDrawer()` itself snapshot-first (`wasHidden` captured before `closeMenus()`, then restored/flipped from the snapshot), so every caller gets correct toggle semantics — close icon, bell, outside-click and modal-open dismissal all re-verified | `toggleDrawer()` snapshots `wasHidden` before `closeMenus()` and derives the new state from it | ✅ user report |

Nov”, JA “11/3”, AR/TR day-first, EN/ES/FR/IT month-first; same-month ranges collapse “13.–24. Okt.”), wired into the i18n layer as **rules — including a new `en` rules array**, so EN formats too (raw ISO must never reach the UI); `i18nPhrase` gained an ISO pre-step so dates embedded in prose/tables/bars format without per-date keys; `trNode`/`i18nWalk`/the MutationObserver were restructured so rules and the phrase pass run in EN as well (fallback to the stored original keeps every other node byte-exact), and plain EN boot now walks once; two build bugs caught by testing: the caption node carries a trailing comma into the capture (fmtDate parse → NaN, fixed with a regex parse that preserves trailing punctuation), and EN initially never reached the phrase pass (raw ISO leaked, fixed); suite now 79 checks — including “no `\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}` survives on the rendered home” and the JA→EN date round-trip | — | — |

| 60 | **The monetary amount assigned to token usage was not configurable** (user question: “Where can I configure the monetary amount assigned to token usage?”; per-gateway-rate option chosen): token cost existed only as read-only showback — hardcoded `cost30d` strings on the `MOCK_GATEWAYS` store ($86.40 / $54.10), surfaced in the Administration → Automation “Gateway usage” card (annotated #20), the Reports KPI (derived from per-run billed records), agent-spend tiles, and the showback-CSV export; the fix makes **the rate ($ per 1M tokens) a first-class per-gateway store field with an editable numeric input on each Automation-table row** — the column is now “Rate → est. cost (30d)”, the gateway's showback cost derives live as tokens × rate (`gwCost`), and editing re-renders both the store and the table (invalid/empty input keeps the prior rate); initial rates ($2.0971 / $5.5204 per 1M) were chosen to **reproduce the shipped costs exactly** at 4 decimals, so no other surface moves; run records keep their historical billed amounts (a rate change is forward pricing, not invoice rewriting) — the Reports KPI and agent-spend tiles therefore intentionally stay run-derived; the Agentic → Gateways registry stays the read-only view per #33 item 9's one-registry-two-views pattern; annotation tip cites #20 item 2 (metered showback); adm43 suite grew 4 checks (derived defaults, aria-labeled inputs, single-gateway re-pricing, store-intact on empty input), 13 suites green | — | — |
| 61 | **Costs were hardcoded dollars regardless of locale** (user request: “can we use the currency that is related to the country with USD the default?”): every monetary amount — gateway showback, run-record costs, the Reports KPI, agent-spend tiles — rendered a `$` string no matter which language was active; the fix adds a **currency layer to the translate-on-render architecture** (`I18N_CURRENCY` + `fmtMoney`): each language maps to the currency of its **primary country (the first flag in LANG_META)** — EN 🇺🇸 USD (default), DE/FR/ES/IT 🇪🇺 EUR, JA 🇯🇵 JPY, TR 🇹🇷 TRY, AR 🇸🇦 SAR — with **demo FX rates** (EUR 0.92, JPY 149, TRY 34.5, SAR 3.75) and per-locale formatting (symbol placement, decimal/thousands separators, JPY with 0 decimals); a money pre-step in `i18nPhrase` converts every `$`-amount text node at render (EN's fx 1.0 reproduces the source byte-exact, so the round-trip guarantee holds), and the row-60 rate inputs follow suit — displayed in the active currency with a currency-tagged aria label, while **edits convert back to the USD store** (type 2 EUR → store 2.173913 USD → ¥13,346 in JA → $89.57 back in EN, all consistent); the German primary-country rule means Swiss users see EUR even though CH uses CHF (documented in the code comment); verified 20 distinct amounts convert on the architect home across all 8 languages with byte-exact EN restoration; i18n suite grew to **86 checks** (EUR-only page audit excluding `<script>`/style source and the EN-only annoTip, KPI conversion, EUR rate inputs, USD store round-trip), 13 suites green | — | — |

| 66 | **The incidents queue had no column sort and no search** (user request: “Can we also make the column sortable and add a search box?”, following row 65's pagination): the queue could be filtered and paginated but not ordered — rows always rendered in app/incident store order — and there was no way to find an incident by text short of reading the queue; the fix mirrors the inventory's widget set exactly: **4 sortable headers** (Application, Incident, Effort, State — `pf-v6-c-table__sort` buttons with `faSvg` up/down indicators and `aria-sort`, effort sorts numerically with missing values last, sorting re-appends rows in sorted order like the inventory and is *not* a filter change so the page is kept, clamped only; Tracker stays plain, mirroring the inventory's non-sortable outcome columns) and an **Enter-applied search box** (“Filter by incident…” — console parity with the inventory's name filter: no live filtering; case-insensitive substring over app name + incident text; a search change IS a filter change so it resets to page 1 and combines with all other filters); the guided empty state (#43) now lists the search among its ways out; a test-only lesson: `#scr-incidents` spans both tabs' tables and the 11-row pool shows 10 on page 1 — two first-run test failures were selector/expectation artifacts, not code bugs; inc42 grew 7 checks (26 total), 13 suites green | — | — |
| 67 | **The incidents State filter's dropdown entries were truncated to the toggle's width** (user report: “On the incidents table the state filter entries are hardly readable because they are truncated”): the shared `.select-wrap .pf-v6-c-menu` rule pinned every dropdown to `left:0; right:0` — i.e. exactly the width of its toggle — so on the incidents queue, whose toggle is only as wide as “State: All”, the entries ellipsized to “State: Tr… / State: In … / State: As…”; the fix is the PatternFly-correct sizing for the rule globally (53 select-wraps): `width:max-content; min-width:100%; max-width:440px` — menus size to their content but never narrower than the toggle, and menus whose items already fit are byte-identical in layout; verified on three screens with open dropdowns (incidents State, inventory row-select, task manager application filter): all fully readable, no horizontal overflow; a CSS-only change — suites re-run green, jsdom does not compute the affected geometry (the defect was invisible to the suites, only the screenshot caught it) | — | — |
| 68 | **The incidents queue rendered “empty” (0 of 0) in every non-English locale** (user report: “The incidents table is ‘empty’” — reproduced with `lang=de`: “0 of 0 incidents” + the guided empty state while EN showed 9 rows): the State filter compared the toggle's *display text* (stripping a literal `State:` prefix and ✓ suffix) against the rows' untranslated `data-state` store values — but since the row-55 i18n work the toggle reads “Status: Alle” in German (“State”→“Status”, “All”→“Alle” in the dict), so no row ever matched; the **Task manager queue had the same latent bug** (its `All`/`Any age` menu items are dict entries too); the fix adopts the inventory's own `invMenuFilter` pattern — canonical `data-value` attributes on the menu items (incidents State ×4; tasks Application ×3 + Age ×3) and `pickOption` now records the picked value on the `select-wrap` (`wrap.dataset.value`), with both filter functions reading that instead of text; two subtle finds along the way: `pickOption` moves `pf-m-selected` among the menu *buttons* while the initial markup puts the class on the *li* (so scanning `li.pf-m-selected` always yields the first item — the wrap-dataset approach sidesteps this), and the defect predates rows 65–67 (any translated session had an empty queue at boot); verified by a new 11-check bilingual suite (EN baseline + DE boot/label/pick/footer for both screens, `verify-i18nfilters`), inc42 grew 2 canonical-value checks (28 total), 14 suites green | — | — |
| 69 | **Untranslated chrome on the incidents view** (user report: “the incident range and the tab names” are not translated): the Triage queue / Insights tab names, the “Assigned to me” chip, the pagination count line (“1 – 9 of 9 incidents” — the “of” and “incidents” words; the numeral range itself is locale-neutral), and the “N per page” label after picking 20/50/100 had no i18n coverage; fixes: dict entries for the tabs + chip in all 7 languages, and **exact-node rules** for the count-line words (`/^ of $/`, `/^ incidents$/` — also `/^ applications$/`, which translates the inventory's identical footer) and `/^(\d+) per page$/` — exact-node rules rather than dict words, because `i18nPhraseKeys` is ALL dict keys and single words like “of” would otherwise be substituted mid-sentence across the whole mock; a second real bug surfaced: the phrase pass's word-boundary class was ASCII+German-umlauts only, so the FR key “Assign”→“Affecter” matched *inside* “Assigné” (é counted as a boundary) and the chip rendered “Affecteré à moi” — the boundary is now a Unicode letter class (`[^\p{L}]` with the `u` flag, safe because dict keys are English and cannot occur inside JA/AR output); the search placeholder stays English like the inventory's (attributes are not walked); verified by a new 18-check suite over DE/FR/JA + EN byte-exactness (`verify-i18ninc`), 14 suites green | — | — |
| 70 | **The search placeholders stayed English** (user follow-up to row 69: “Can you also translate the search placeholder?” — row 69 had noted the attribute gap as a known limit): the text-node walk can't see attributes, so the incidents “Filter by incident…” and the inventory's “Filter by name…” inputs were untranslated in all 7 locales; the fix marks both inputs with a `data-i18n-ph` attribute (its value is the EN key — EN falls back to it byte-exact when the dict is empty) and `setLang` now sets `placeholder = i18nDict[key] |  | key` for every `[data-i18n-ph]` element, so the placeholders translate on boot and on every language switch (DE “Nach Vorfall filtern …” / “Nach Name filtern …”, plus AR/ES/FR/JA/IT/TR); a suite-editing stumble: the new checks were first inserted after the loop where the test's `d` binding is in its dead zone — moved inside the loop; verify-i18ninc grew 3 checks (21 total), 14 suites green |

`2px`→3px slight change) / `--rh-border-radius-sharp` (the `0` values incl. the mixed forms) / literal `0` kept inside multi-corner lists, font-sizes → `--rh-font-size-body-text-*` nearest mapping (10–12.5px→xs=12, 13–15px→sm=14, 16px→md, 17–18px→lg, 20px→xl, 22/26px→2xl=24; the 9px `.footnote summary::after` caret glyph kept raw as icon-size chrome, same category as the 1em `svg.fas` sizing), standalone `opacity:.55`→`--rh-opacity-50` (lower on tie — disabled content should recede) and the switch-toggle color alphas `.22`/`.65`→`rgba(255,255,255,var(--rh-opacity-20/60))` (percentage alpha via var, valid CSS); verified by headless-Chrome computed-style probes (all rendered shadows are now exactly the two token values `0 8px 24px 3px rgba(21,21,21,.35)`/`0 6px 8px 2px rgba(21,21,21,.3)`, `.stackBar` radius 3px, tokens resolve from `:root`, footnote caret stays 9px), before/after inventory screenshots (dense table legible, chips/labels slightly larger, no layout breakage), parse-check, and the full 16-suite regression battery (all green) | — | — |

Static + runtime audit of the new specs — **3 fixes**: (1) **Dialog focus management** (RHDS Dialog: focus moves inside on open, Tab is trapped, focus restores to the opener on close): `openModal` now records the opener (`modalOpener`) and focuses the modal box's first focusable control; a `modalTrap` Tab-wrap handler is wired into the global keydown listener (wrap last→first / shift-Tab first→last / focus pulled inside if outside); `modalRestore()` is called from `closeModals`, `closeAppForm`, `closeAppModal`, and the global Escape handler — restore re-queries by id (`focusToggle`) because openers can be re-rendered between open and close; verified in headless Chrome (focus inside modal on open `modalFocusInside:true`, trap wraps last→first `trapWrappedToFirst:true`, Escape restores focus to the opener). (2) **Menu-dropdown focus management** (RHDS Menu: focus → first menu item on expand; Escape/selection closes and returns focus to the toggle): `menuOpened(m, btn)` is called by all four masthead menu toggles (language, help, tenancy, arch-level) after opening and focuses the first `.pf-v6-c-menu__item`; `wireMenuRestore` (delegated click listener on langMenu/tenancyMenu/archLevelMenu/helpMenu) restores focus to the toggle on item selection (unless the selection opened a dialog — the dialog keeps focus); the global Escape handler restores to `menuOpener`; the typeahead select-wrap filter menus are documented as accepted deviation (focus stays in their input per PF6 typeahead convention; they now DO close on Escape — select-wrap menus added to the global Escape handler); verified in Chrome (focus lands on first item `focusInMenu:true`, selection restores `afterLangSelect:"toggle"`, Escape restores `afterEscape:"toggle"`; a first probe round on the arch-level switcher as padmin produced false alarms — the toggle is hidden for that persona/screen so `focus()` correctly no-ops, and the clipboard promise settles asynchronously — resolved by probing the always-visible language switcher with delayed captures; `focusToggle` re-queries by id since switcher re-renders can replace the opener). (3) **Code-block Copy action** (RHDS Code block anatomy: header carries a copy-to-clipboard action alongside Expand/Collapse): all 4 code blocks (2 in `tpl-drawer-qyaml`/`tpl-drawer-wfyaml` templates with grouped Copy+Expand buttons, 2 short blocks in `tpl-drawer-ticket`/`tpl-drawer-task`) got a `pf-m-link pf-m-small` Copy button wired to `codeBlockCopy()` (clipboard write via `navigator.clipboard.writeText` with sync-throw and rejection handling; label swaps to "Copied!"/"Copy failed!" and reverts after 1.6s); verified in Chrome on the cloned drawer content (button present/enabled/visible, click fires, feedback swaps — headless shows "Copy failed!" for the untrusted synthetic click; Also investigated the "empty middle" impression in the KPI cards: it is PF6's sanctioned card behavior — `.pf-v6-c-card__body:not(.pf-m-no-fill){flex:1 1 auto}` fills cards stretched to the grid row height; at the real 1600px viewport the stretch slack is small (row 220px, natural heights 184–220px, ≤36px slack) — **not** a defect. **Probe landmine (recorded)**: headless-Chrome probes WITHOUT `--window-size` default to an 800px viewport — the first measurement pass produced misleading artifacts (94px-wide cards wrapping into a 464px row) that vanished at 1600px; always pass `--window-size` and re-measure before diagnosing. | 102 | **"The toggle button on the upper left breaks the page."** (user bug report — the masthead ☰ nav toggle): clicking the sidebar collapse toggle crushed the main content into a ~64px vertical sliver. Fix: `body.sidebar-collapsed .pf-v6-c-page__main{grid-column:2;}` pins main to the second track while collapsed (`display:none` kept — width-0 hiding would leave the nav links focusable); verified with a computed-style round-trip probe (main 1690px/col auto → 1920px/col 2 → 1690px restored) and a headless-Chrome screenshot of the collapsed state (sidebar gone, content full-width, layout intact); parse-check + full 18-suite battery green. Durable rule: hiding a grid sibling with `display:none` changes auto-placement for everything after it — pin explicit column placement on the remaining items when collapsing a named grid track | — | — |

| 207 | **Wave burndown chart** (user request "add a Wave burndown chart", 2026-11-18; both placements + remaining-effort metric chosen from options; user refinement same session: "Can we use a bar chart instead?" — the actual series draws as **columns**, one per analysis-run point, the ideal stays a dashed guide line): per-wave burndown renders in a dedicated **Burndown tab** (7th lens) in each wave-detail tab bar (waves 3/4 — the only waves with tab bars; member-less waves 1/2 keep the inline honest dash, there is no tab bar to host it) and an all-waves aggregate card joins Reports → Trends (#14 anno). **Derived, not decorative**: the drawn series is anchored to the live member rows — the first point IS the live total (`data-pts` sum), the last IS the live remaining (succeeded `data-state` pts subtracted), and intermediate snapshots sit on the analysis-run cadence at seeded shape factors of the live done-share, so the chart cannot drift from the table it summarizes (asserted: caption `<rem>/<total>` equals the recomputed live facts). Wave windows ride new `data-win` attributes on the gantt bars — the bar *text* is ISO in source but the i18n phrase pass reformats it at runtime ("Oct 13–24"), so the chart reads the attribute, not the label. Honest states: wave 1 (no sampled rows) renders the dash, wave 2 likewise (the mock's inventory assigns members only to waves 3/4 — no synthetic zero-burn for done-but-unsampled waves), wave 3 draws past its window end (axis extends to the snapshot date, "past the window end" in the aria-label — slippage visible), wave 4 renders in-flight because two seeded members already succeeded (the "planned" pill is seed fiction the chart refuses to inherit). Aggregate bar colors are the waves' own pill hues read live from the lane labels (`burnPillHue` → nonstatus tokens, row-206 pairs), offset side-by-side where wave windows share dates; per-wave bars are value-labeled (last point emphasized) and colored by state. **Polish pass (user request "redesign so it looks nicer", polished-bars option chosen)**: nice-scale y gridlines with labeled ticks (step rounding 1/2/2.5/5×10^k, so 1803 pts reads against 500/1000/1500/2000), rounded-top bar paths clamped fully inside the plot (endpoint bars no longer clip the y-label gutter or bleed past the right edge — a screenshot-caught defect), native `<title>` hover tooltips per bar (date — pts remaining), a subtle gradient area under the ideal trajectory (PF token color, `--rh-opacity-20` → 0 — no decimal opacities, row 80), run-date ticks on the x axis, and the aggregate's mid snapshot tick is dropped when it would collide with a window-end tick (Nov 18 vs Nov 21, 8%-of-span guard). **Line-chart refinement (user request "use a patternfly line chart instead")**: the actual series renders as a solid `ChartLine` with a ringed `ChartScatter` dot per analysis-run point (aggregate: one line per wave, per-wave dashed ideals); the hand-rolled fallback mirrors the same line idiom (solid polyline + titled dots). Always-visible point labels were dropped in BOTH renderers — Victory clips labels that sit above the plot area (the first burndown point is always at the domain max, so its label was unrecoverable even with domain padding), and the values remain available through the Voronoi hover tooltip plus the live caption. **Compact pass (user request "make this line chart more compact")**: viewBox 560×300 → 720×210 with tightened chart padding and domain padding (both renderers) — the panel width now renders the chart at roughly half the previous height, and the aggregate card fits its chart without dominating the tab. **Tooltip pass (user report: first point's tooltip clipped, other points showed the information twice)**: two Victory mechanics — the clip came from the earlier label experiment having disabled `constrainToVisibleArea` (restored; the tooltip now clamps inside the chart bounds at the domain-edge first point), and the double tooltip came from the voronoi container activating two series at the same coordinates (the ringed-dot `ChartScatter` overlays every `ChartLine` point, and the ideal line shares the first point with the actual series — the first burndown point IS total on both). Fix: `voronoiBlacklist` excludes the dot and ideal series from hover activation, so exactly one tooltip fires per point. Verified with a synthetic-hover probe in headless Chrome (mousemove at the tick coordinates): 1 tooltip per point, bounds inside the chart — the fallback renderer is unaffected (its `<title>` tooltips are native and single). **Light-panel + UI-font pass (user request)**: all in-SVG labels use the PF6 body font size (14px — the size the rest of the UI body text runs at; family is inherited Red Hat Text). **White-surface pass (user request "grey background while all other tabs are using white")**: the initial secondary-background panel was removed, but the gray persisted — the painter is the wave-detail expansion well itself (`.wave-exec-detail` carries secondary background by design; the other tabs read white only because their tables/cards cover it, and the transparent chart let the well show through). Final state: `.wave-burn` carries its own `--pf-t--global--background--color--primary--default` (white) surface — full-bleed like the other tabs' tables, token-flipping in the dark scheme. The larger labels re-introduced a top-left collision (y "2000" vs the first bar's "1803", both wider at 14px) — the top gridline label is skipped when the chart's first bar, clamped to the left edge, sits at the max (a burndown always starts at its total, so the bar's own value label is the top reference). **PatternFly react-charts pass (user request "use the patternfly bar chart"; PF charts are React-only — verified against the v6 docs, no HTML implementation exists)**: the chart now mounts the real `@patternfly/react-charts` (ChartBar/ChartLine/ChartAxis/ChartGroup/ChartVoronoiContainer) via a **self-hosted esbuild bundle** (`vendor/pf-charts.iife.min.js`, 657 KB — React 18.3.1 + react-charts 8.7.0 + Victory 37.3.6 as one classic-script IIFE). CDN delivery was tried first and failed empirically: esm.sh returns 408 (build timeout) on the react-charts graph, jsDelivr +esm 404s — and ESM module scripts also have no jsdom support, so the self-hosted classic script keeps `file://` working, offline-tolerant, and suite-testable. Dual-mode by design: `mountBurnChart` prefers the React mount (live-anchored data unchanged) and falls back to the hand-rolled `burnSvgLines` when `PFChartKit` is absent — the jsdom suites (which strip all external scripts) exercise the fallback and still assert the data contract; headless probes assert the real thing (`pf:true`, `pf-v6-c-chart`, Victory svg). Landing defects caught by screenshots and fixed: the `.wave-burn svg{width:100%}` scaling rule also stretched the legend's 20×8 swatch svgs (wrapping the legend — scoped to `.burn-canvas`), and Victory draws all tick labels so the top y-label "2000" collided with the first bar's "1803" (the fallback's guard ported to the tick values). Victory paints colors via SVG attributes where `var()` does not resolve, so colors are RESOLVED token values at render time (`burnTok`) and `applyScheme` re-mounts every chart through `window.__burnRemount` — dark scheme verified by screenshot (light crayons on the dark panel). Tooltips come from `ChartVoronoiContainer` (date — value); axis tick labels are ISO dates (data, EN-stable); legend and caption stay walker-translated HTML outside the React root. `verify-burn.js` re-pointed for the host-carried panel and the line idiom (20 assertions green on the fallback); battery 35/35 (2026-11-19, post-row-209 state) #32 kept: legend ("Ideal"/"Remaining effort"/wave names) and captions carry the state in text, never color alone. i18n: 4 dict keys ×9 ("Wave burndown", "Ideal", "Remaining effort", "pts remaining"); ISO axis labels localize via the phrase pass. Two build defects caught and fixed en route: the Reports card initially landed inside `panel-rpt-deps` (the rfind anchor crossed the panel boundary — Artifact inventory lives there) and the pill-hue `slice(4)` produced an invalid token (invisible strokes). 3. **Top-N honesty.** Where data is sampled (incident groups "top 3 of 137", affected apps "top 4
   of 18", agent runs "1–3 of 16") the sampling is labeled in the UI rather than hidden. The same
   applies to dependency inventories: payments-api (5 of 214 artifacts) and invoicing (2) are the
   sampled examples, and apps without sampled data say so and hand off to the Dependencies view
   instead of showing an empty table.
4. **Detail identity follows the clicked row.** The incident detail's story scaffolding (rule
   transparency, hint verification, triage history, role-gated verbs) is one canonical pattern,
   but its identity fields — application, incident, effort, triage state, tracker outcome, rule
   path — are now assembled from the clicked row, the same pattern the app drawer already used
   (#33 item 8). Before this (defect #14), every incident row opened the canonical invoicing
   story regardless of which application's row was clicked.
5. **Reference integrity: two accepted classes of dead-ends.** The dead-end audit (defects #10
   and #12) wired every entity reference that has a correct in-mock target — including the
   runtime-generated rows the static scan initially missed. What intentionally remains plain text:
   **tracker keys (MIG-412/418/425)** — they reference an external system, and the only in-mock
   ticket drawer holds MIG-425's failure story; pointing the others at it would present wrong
   data (the real product would deep-link to the tracker, #37); **read-only record rows** —
   triage audit-log entries (#33 item 1: the trail is for reading, not navigating), ticket
   attempt history, agent token-accounting and policy rows, which are records rather than
   references; **self-references** (drawer and modal titles, artifact tables in the Dependencies
   views, the Rule-coverage tab's own rule path, YAML/OpenAPI excerpts); **references with an
   adjacent way out** (the Reports provenance cell names the archetype beside a "view group
   assessment" link; the incident modal's cohort line beside "open cohort"); and **workflow scope
   labels** (`archetype: payments-api` tags, which are metadata, consistent with filter chips not
   being navigation).
6. **Column alignment is presentation, but misalignment reads as wrong data.** The Applications
   table's header was missing the checkbox column's `<th>` (defect #16), so every label sat one
   column left — most misleadingly, "Effort #4" sat over the Analysis cell, whose "Running 61% ·
   23 incidents" reads like a status. The underlying data was never wrong, but a header that
   mislabels its column destroys trust in the numbers beneath it as effectively as a data error.
   Fixed and geometry-verified (left-edge deltas ≤ 4px); every other checkbox table in the mock
   was swept for the same mismatch — none found.

## 6. Open items

- **UC-M2 / UC-A7 / UC-AD1 / UC-X6** are content-verified but not script-asserted; the mock's
  buttons are intentionally inert (`onclick="this.blur()"`) where the suggestion only requires the
  surface, not working state changes.
- **Suggestion 35 (automated UI quality gate)** is itself unimplemented: this document's two
  harnesses are its manual proof-of-concept. Follow-up: commit the jsdom + Chrome harnesses as
  scripts and run them in CI.
- **Filtering by incident category from "Combined risks"** is deliberately *not* a navigation
  (Applications has no category dimension); expansion in place is the designed answer. Revisit if
  a category dimension is added to the inventory.

## 7. Parity audit vs. the live MTA 8.3.3 console (2026-11-12; re-audited 2026-11-17)

**Re-audit (2026-11-17, headless-Chrome crawl of the same sandbox instance, admin/admin).**
The instance reports **8.3.3** in its About dialog — the same baseline, so every delta below is an
audit-coverage finding, not upstream drift. Method: crawl of all 17 navigable routes (per-view table
column sets, toolbar verbs, pagination, console/API errors), probing of unlisted routes, and the
application create-modal's form fields.

- **Navigation structure**: the console's nav is flat and grouped — **Migration** (Applications,
  Archetypes, Migration waves) · **Analysis Results** (Reports, Issues, Insights, Dependencies) ·
  **Agentic** (Agent runs, Agents, Skills, Workflows, Workflow runs, Gateways) · **Configuration**
  (Analysis Profiles, Controls, Custom migration targets) · plus Task Manager. **There is no
  Administration mode switch**: the six access/identity views (Users, Tokens, Roles, Scopes,
  Identities, Source platforms) still resolve by URL but have **no nav entry at all** (§7.2). The
  mock's flat six-view + Administration-hub consolidation (#67) is now *ahead* of upstream on
  discoverability, not behind it.
- **Column sets re-verified against the mock**: Applications (Name/Business Service/Assessment/
  Review/Analysis/Tags/Effort + row actions — 123 applications upstream) ✓; Archetypes ✓ (now
  with a Profiles column); Migration waves (Name/Start/End/Applications/**Stakeholders**/Status) ✓
  (the mock's WP10 restore is upstream-true); Task Manager (ID/Application/Status/Kind/Priority/
  Created By) ✓; Reports questionnaire grid ✓; Issues/Insights/Dependencies ✓ (WP9 split and #24
  inventory confirmed); Controls: 4 tabs (Stakeholders/Stakeholder groups/Job functions/Business
  services) ✓; Agents (Name/Image/Gateways/**Skills/Params**/Ready/Age) ✓ — the mock's agents
  table matches column-for-column; Skills ✓ (upstream adds a second skill-*cards* table — the mock
  renders skills as cards, recorded in §7.1); Workflows ✓; Workflow runs (Name/Workflow/
  Application/Phase/Stages/Age/Duration — the mock's record superset adds State); Gateways ✓;
  Agent runs (Name/Agent/Application/Phase/Reason/Age/Duration — the mock's record superset adds
  the #50 event log and #20 accounting).
- **New upstream surface disclosed by this crawl**: an **Analysis Profiles** view
  (`/analysis-profiles`, Name/Description, "Create new") — not present in the 2026-11-12 audit
  rows; the mock referenced analysis profiles only in its Hub-API scope catalog. Recorded as a §7.1
  gap and closed the same day (defect row 130 — Config → Analysis profiles card).
- **Application create-form delta**: upstream's form now also carries **Source platform +
  Source platform coordinates** and an **Asset repository** block (Repository type/Branch/Root
  path) beyond the fields §7.1 cites — folded into that row's omitted-fields list.
- **Console hygiene**: one failed resource on /applications and one PatternFly component warning
  on /reports ("Cards with a selectable input must have either the selected…") — the 2026-11-12
  audit's "zero console errors" no longer holds verbatim, but both are cosmetic; zero failed API
  calls on any view's own data.

### 7.0 Re-audit vs the 2026-11-12 rows

All §7.1 rows marked **implemented** (WP1–WP9) were re-verified against the live surface: each
crawled column set matches the mock's table (supersets only, listed above); the Agentic registries
carry the same create/guarded-delete/"Delete finished (N)" verbs; and the per-application detail
tabs (WP8) are unchanged upstream. The only new surface is Analysis Profiles (new §7.1 row).



Method: headless-Chrome login (provided sandbox credentials) against the live console at
`mta-openshift-mta.apps.ocp.2xhp7.sandbox1251.opentlc.com` (SPA reports `VERSION: 8.3.3`, 123
applications, 494 tasks); every top-level route crawled for tabs, columns, card titles, toolbar
actions, and row-action menus. The mock is a **suggestion-driven redesign**, not a console
replica — this audit lists what the mock does not model.

### 7.1 Real console surfaces with no mock counterpart

| Upstream surface (8.3.3) | Evidence from crawl | Mock status |

### 7.2 Surfaces the mock deliberately re-scopes (not gaps)

- **Migration waves**: upstream is a simple planner (Name/Start/End/Applications/**Stakeholders**/Status);
  the mock implements suggestions #46/#47 on top (gates, evidence, effort aggregates, tickets,
  planned-vs-actual) and has **restored the Stakeholders column** (parity-plan WP10 — it was the
  only upstream field lost in a modeled surface; wave rows now carry stakeholders again, Wave 4
  honestly labeled "pending staffing").
- **Reports**: upstream `/reports` is the questionnaire answer grid; the mock's Reports screen is
  the #22/#32/#14 portfolio analysis. Upstream per-app analysis reports live in the app-detail
  Reports tab, which the mock replaces with run comparison (#1).
- **Issues/Incidents**: upstream list (Issue/Category/Source/Target(s)/Effort/Affected
  applications); the mock's Incidents screen adds the triage/effort/verification story (#33 et al.).
- **Home**: upstream has no dashboard; the mock's Home is suggestion #36's persona workspace.
- **Access management (Users / Tokens / Roles / Scopes)**: the re-audit (2026-11-17) found these views still resolve by URL (`/users`, `/tokens`, `/roles`, `/scopes`) but carry **no navigation entry** in the 8.3.3 console — upstream itself has demoted them below the sidebar. The mock models the *operational* identity side (the Credentials view, #15) and an Access sub-tab; role/scope *administration* stays out of scope (they are read-only inspectors — see the refinement below), while **token administration landed with defect row 129** (2026-11-17): the Access tab's Tokens card mirrors the live `/tokens` column set plus search/kind filter/show-expired/sortable/paged browsing and single+bulk revocation (audited, RBAC-gated) — at parity modulo token *issuance*, which the live UI does not offer either (revocation is the management verb the console exposes). Refinement (live-data check, 2026-11-17): the live Roles view defines exactly **one role per persona** — `admin` (366 scopes), `architect` (179), `migrator` (82), `project-manager` (56), `addon` (74) — and the Scopes view is only the flattened `resource:verb` catalog, so the mock's five personas with their hard-coded scope sets (`RBAC` const) *are* the role definitions; the unmodeled views are inspectors of that fixed mapping, not a modeling gap.

### 7.3 Reading the audit

The §7.1 audit is now fully closed: every real-console surface without a mock counterpart at
the time of the audit has landed — archetype management (WP2), the assessment/review loop
(WP3), application CRUD (WP4), Controls (WP5), the task manager (WP6), the registries (WP7),
the per-application detail tabs (WP8), the Agentic section (WP1), and the Insights split
(WP9, the user's split decision; the merge-into-Issues argument remains documented as an
upstream-action alternative, not argued by the mock). What remains unmodeled is only the
deliberately-scoped depth listed in the §7.1 rows (fields and sub-surfaces the mock's forms and
tables omit) — every remaining gap is a *modeled-surface simplification*, not a missing
capability. Everything the mock models is anchored in upstream data (waves, applications,
issues, insights, dependencies, agents) — nothing is invented outside MTA's domain. Conversely, everything the mock *does* model is anchored in upstream data
(waves, applications, issues, dependencies, agents) — nothing is invented outside MTA's domain.
